## Document 062: 2026年7-9月期GDP予測（1次速報） ～前期比年率＋0.4％と予想。成長率は鈍化も、景気回復の流れは崩れず～

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026 年 10 月30 日

1/3 
2026 年 7-9 月期 GDP 予測（1 次速報） 
～前期比年率＋0.4％と予想。成長率は鈍化も、景気回復の流れは崩れず～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

小幅ながら４四半期連続のプラス成長を予想、景気回復基調は維持 
11 月16 日に公表される2026 年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率を前期比年率＋0.4％（前期比＋
0.1％）と予測する。４四半期連続のプラス成長になるだろう1。 
物価高による下押しや耐久財需要の反動に加え、天候不順の影響もあって個人消費が減少する
一方、設備投資が高い伸びになることが下支えになる見込みである。設備投資の伸びには特殊要
因も一部含まれているが、それを除いても堅調だったと考えられる。個人消費が弱含むなかでも
プラス成長を維持するとみられることは、企業部門の底堅さが景気を下支えしていることを示し
ている。過去３四半期に比較的高い成長が続いた後であることも踏まえれば、今回の減速をもっ
て景気の基調が弱まったと判断する必要はないだろう。家計部門の弱さは懸念材料だが、企業部
門の底堅さは維持されており、景気は引き続き緩やかな回復基調にあると評価できる。

輸入増による成長率押し下げと公的在庫のプラス寄与は表裏一体 
７-９月期のＧＤＰを評価する際には、原油輸入と公的在庫の動きをセットで捉える必要があ
る。４-６月期は、中東情勢の悪化によって原油輸入が激減したことが、ＧＤＰ統計上は輸入減を
通じて成長率を押し上げた。一方、輸入の減少による国内供給の不足を補うために国家備蓄原油
が放出され、公的在庫の取り崩しが進んだ。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.5％Pt
（前期比年率寄与度▲2.1％Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し下げた。輸入減による成長率の押し上げ
は、公的在庫の減少と表裏一体の関係にあった。 
７-９月期は、これと逆の動きが生じるとみられる。政府・民間による代替調達の進展によって
原油輸入が回復するため、統計上は輸入増が成長率の押し下げ要因になる。一方、輸入の回復に
伴って国家備蓄原油の取り崩しは一服しており、公的在庫の減少幅は前期に比べて大きく縮小す
るとみられる。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.4％Pt（前期比年率寄与度＋1.5％
Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し上げることが予想される。

1 10 月 30 日時点で入手可能な経済指標を元に作成している。国際収支や家計調査等、今後公表される経済指標の結果
を踏まえて予測値を修正する可能性がある。

### Page 2

2/3 
輸入増による成長率の押し下げと公的在庫変動による押し上げは、ともに原油の供給環境が改
善したことに伴う動きである。両者を個別に景気の強弱として評価することは適切ではなく、一
体として見るべきだろう。 
むしろ重要なのは、代替調達の進展によって国家備蓄への依存を減らしながら必要な原油を確
保できる状況になり、資源不足が生産活動を大きく制約するリスクが低下していることである。
ＧＤＰ統計上、輸入の増加は成長率の押し下げ要因となるものの、経済実態としては国内で利用
可能な資源の供給回復を意味している。この点は、７-９月期の日本経済を評価するうえで、むし
ろ前向きに捉えるべきだろう。

需要項目別の動向 
個人消費は前期比▲0.2％と小幅減少を予想する。８～９月に各地で相次いだ大雨や台風などの
天候不順が外出機会やサービス消費を抑制した可能性がある。また、４-６月期には自動車やエア
コンなどの耐久財販売が増加したが、７-９月期にはこうした一時的な押し上げ効果が剥落した。
加えて、物価高による節約志向が根強いなか、食料品や日用品などの非耐久財消費にも弱さがみ
られる。高い賃上げや底堅い雇用環境は下支え要因となっているものの、天候不順という一時的
な要因もあって個人消費は総じて力強さを欠く結果になったとみられる。 
設備投資は前期比＋2.0％と、高い伸びを予想する。４-６月期には特許権の海外譲渡に伴う研
究開発投資の減少という特殊要因があったが、７-９月期はその影響が剥落することが押し上げ要
因になった面はある。もっとも、こうした特殊要因を除いても設備投資は明確に増加したと考え
られ、底堅い動きとなっている。企業収益が高水準を維持するなか、人手不足に対応した省力化
投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資への需要は根強く、企業の設備投資意欲は引き続き強い。設備投
資計画も高い伸びとなっており、設備投資は均してみれば緩やかな増加基調にあると判断してよ
いだろう。 
なお、特許権の海外への譲渡により、テクニカルに４-６月期はサービス輸出が上振れ、設備投
資が下振れたが、７-９月期はその剥落によりサービス輸出が下振れ、設備投資が上振れとなる見
込みである。両者は相殺し合うため、成長率全体でみれば概ねニュートラルとなる。 
輸出は前期比＋1.2％と４四半期連続の増加を予想する。４-６月期に大口要因によって研究開
発サービスの受取が急増した分が剥落することで、サービス輸出が減少する一方、財輸出が高い
伸びとなり、財貨・サービス輸出全体では増加を維持したとみられる。半導体製造装置などの資
本財輸出が好調だった。地域別では、ＥＵ向けが低調だった一方、ＡＳＥＡＮ、ＮＩＥｓ向けの
資本財輸出が伸びている。また、４-６月期に急減した中東向け輸出が持ち直したことも押し上げ
につながっている。 
輸入は前期比＋3.3％と大幅な増加が予想される。４-６月期には資源輸入の激減により輸入が
大幅に減少していたが、代替調達の進展により７-９月期は明確に持ち直した。この結果、外需寄
与度は前期比▲0.3％Pt（前期比年率▲1.3％Pt）になったと予想される。 
ただし、この外需寄与度のマイナスは、資源輸入の回復に伴う輸入の増加によって生じるもの

なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
である。その裏側では公的在庫変動が前期比＋0.4％ポイント、前期比年率では＋1.5％ポイント
の大幅プラス寄与になると見込まれる。両者は併せて評価する必要がある。 
民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.1％Ptと予想する。製品在庫はプラスが予想される一方、流通
在庫の下押しが大きくなるとみられる。 
住宅投資は前期比▲0.9％と、２四半期連続の減少を予想する。建設コストの上昇が下押し要因
となっており、住宅投資を取り巻く環境は引き続き厳しい。また、公共投資も同▲1.5％と減少を
予想するほか、政府消費についても、４-６月期の高い伸びの反動から前期比▲0.3％と減少を見
込む。

### Page 1

Faraday

Valuation remains stretched; timely 
revenue delivery to be key watchpoint

Reiterate Rating: UNDERPERFORM | PO: 120.00 TWD | Price: 191.50 TWD

3Q26 sales beat on record MCU mass production revenue  
Faraday’s 3Q26 sales was +11% QoQ, above BofAe/street of -7%/-3% QoQ and its 
guidance of -single-digit% QoQ, mainly driven by strong MP growth (+19.6% QoQ) on 
record-high MCU sales and accelerating ASIC shipments, coupled with continued IP 
revenue strength more than offsetting deferred NRE revenue recognition. 3Q26 GM 
dropped by 270 bps to 42.5% due to product mix, while increased Opex from salary 
adjustments brought 3Q26 EPS to NT$1.07 (BofAe/Street NT$1.27/0.66).  
IP and NRE strength support 4Q26 margin improvement 
4Q26 sales is guided to remain flat QoQ, as growth in IP and NRE revenue offset decline 
in MP revenue on lower MCU shipments following a high base in 3Q26. With sustained 
strength in IP segment underpinned by higher wafer pricing and utilization, along with 
NRE milestone delivery, 4Q26 GM should edge up to 43.7% on better mix, in our view.  
High-end MCU and advanced projects drive 2027 growth  
We now forecast Faraday's 2026/2027 sales to -27%/+36% YoY, supported by healthy 
demand for high-end MCU and gradual ramp in advanced projects. While the design 
change from 4nm to 2nm for a US customer’s ASIC project should bring greater contract 
value, Faraday’s capability to deliver on schedule and achieve timely revenue recognition 
remains key watchpoint. On higher advanced packaging/nodes projects contribution 
through 2027, GM should decline slightly from 44.5% in 2026 to 42.9% in 2027. 
Reiterate Underperform with unchanged NT$120 PO 
We lower our 2026 EPS from NT$4.05 to NT$3.19 on higher Opex and trim 2027-28 
EPS by 3-10% as we remain cautious on the timing of NRE/MP recognition for advanced 
projects given the increased design complexity and constrained OSAT capacity. We 
reiterate Underperform rating with unchanged PO of NT$120 on 17x 2027E P/E. Current 
valuation appears stretched with shares trading at 28x '27 P/E (vs. historical 13-42x).

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Faraday Technology Corp. was established in 1993 and 
listed in 1999. The company is an affiliate of the second 
biggest semiconductor logic foundry (UMC) in Taiwan, and 
has been the main provider to UMC's IP library. Faraday is 
also known for its total solution in ASIC (customized chips) 
which includes chip-design and turnkey mass production, 
on top of its IP offering. The company has a diversified end-
market exposure, including internet of things, factory 
automation, solar, microcontrollers etc.

Investment Rationale 
We have an Underperform rating on Faraday, as we are 
wary of the slower for longer business outlook in mature 
node projects, while its collaboration with non-TSMC 
foundries bears the uncertainty.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Faraday (FDYTF) 
Our PO NT$120 is derived from 17x 2027E P/E, which is at low-to-mid-end of the 
company's historical valuation range. In our view, the multiple is backed by gross margin 
expansion from 27% in 2025 to over 40% in 2026/27 while revenue would slow down 
from a peak in 2025.

Upside risks: (1) faster-than-expected progress in new businesses including advanced 
nodes and advanced packaging, and (2) a better margin profile thanks to more stringent 
opex and enhanced value-add to customers

Downside risks: (1) downturn in foundry business cycle, given that the firm adopts a 
cost-plus model for turnkey (mass production) business, and (2) slower qualification and 
ramp-up of the yield rate for advanced packaging business

Update
3Q26 earnings beat on better margins: See Results Summary
4Q26 revenue seen rising QoQ: Management guided for  4Q26 revenue to grow
QoQ, driven by power related products in Cloud & AIoT. IT/CE should continue to
decline YoY, despite strong growth from the LEO (low earth orbit). Optoelectronics
should grow by 5-10% YoY. Overall, we estimate revenue will grow by 5% QoQ in
4Q26 and GM will expand further to 26.1% (+0.2ppt QoQ) on a more favorable

revenue mix.
2027 expected to be another solid year: Looking to 2027, management holds an
optimistic view, guiding for the Cloud & AIoT business to grow by ~70% YoY,
Optoelectronics to grow by ~10% YoY and IT/CE business to stay roughly flat YoY.
AI remains the major driver, and should benefit from the growing AI deployment and
the spec upgrade of power-related products.
800VDC power rack under qualification: Lite-On sent its 800VDC power rack for

qualification in August and will have the next round of integrated system
qualification in November, aiming to be MP ready in 1Q27. That said, shipments will
depend upon customer deployment plans. Management expects only small volume
shipments in 1H27, and said visibility into the adoption in the new generation ASIC/
GPU platform from 2H27 remains to be seen, consistent with the consensus that
HVDC is more of a 2028 story at the earliest.
More offerings in the pipeline: Management sent DC/DC module samples to CSP

for qualification and expects to see some revenue contribution from 3Q27 (not
meaningful, though). Its 120KW CDU has obtained qualification in 3Q26 and should
see more volume from 4Q26, with the next generation 220KW CDU currently in the
development stage. In September 2026, it invested in a 25% stake in DCX, a liquid
cooling solution supplier in Poland, to strengthen its capability in the cooling space .
Stay EW: We think near-term sentiment should be relatively positive given the solid

3Q26 results, especially after the recent share price correction. Looking ahead, we
think Lite-On will grow along with the AI capex cycle, driven by its power-related
offerings, though execution and progress with new customers is worth monitoring.

Results Summary
3Q26 beat on better margins
Off revenue of NT$57,403mn (+9% QoQ/+28% YoY), GM contracted by 1.3ppt QoQ to
25.9% (vs. MSe/consensus at 24.6%/25.4%). The sequential decline was due to a high base
in 2Q26, which was elevated due to some one-offs. Opex was well controlled at NT
$6,211mn (opex ratio 10.8%), lower than our estimate of NT$6,851mn (opex ratio 11.5%)
and consensus of NT$7,097mn (opex ratio 11.8%)
Operating profit came in at NT$8,655mn (+6% QoQ/+85% YoY; OPM 15.1%),11% and 5%

higher than MSe of NT$7,807mn (OPM 13.1%) and consensus of NT$8,210mn (OPM
13.6%), respectively.
With non-op gains of NT$1,142mn (including FX gain of NT$734mn), net profit was NT
$7,429mn (+4% QoQ/+60% YoY; EPS NT$3.26), 18% and 11% above MSe (NT$6,313mn;
EPS NT$2.77) and consensus (NT$6,717mn; EPS NT$2.92).

### Page 6

M
Update
6
Valuation Methodology
Our price target (base case scenario value) is NT$285 (prior NT$315), derived from 15x
2027-28e P/E (previously 17x): We use price/earnings as our valuation methodology
because we believe it appropriately captures Lite-On's earnings growth potential. We use
a lower target multiple to reflect more conservative sentiment toward the AI space in the
near term. We believe our target P/E multiple of 15x is justified by an earnings CAGR of

50% seen over 2025-28, which implies a PEG of 0.3. This is lower than the average of 1.6
in 2020-25, when the stock was trading at an average P/E of 14x for a 9% earnings CAGR.
Bull case scenario value – NT$490 (previously NT$500): We apply a 25x P/E multiple
(previously 27x) to our average base case estimate for  2027-28. This scenario assumes
better-than-expected margin expansion, driven by operating efficiency and accelerating
production automation, while new business revenue comes in earlier than expected.

Strength from cloud computing and auto electronics is greater than expected.
Bear case scenario value – NT$190 (previously NT$200):  We apply a 10x P/E multiple
(previously 11x) to our average base case estimate for  2027-28. This scenario assumes
weaker-than-expected operating efficiency improvement, while pricing competition
increases amid macro volatility; auto electronics takes longer to pick up.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

公司簡介  
營邦企業股份有限公司創立於 1996 年 ， 為全
球伺服器與儲存系統製造商領導者之一。 產品
發展方向由工業電腦延伸至系統散熱解決方
案，再擴展至高密度系統解決方案。近年來，
營邦深耕企業級伺服器與儲存解決方案領域，
專注於高效能運算 、 資安應用及資料中心基礎
架構之產品研發與製造，產品範疇涵蓋類別主
要包含：伺服器機箱、存儲系統、系統平台、
產業設備等產品。

2

營運展望 
1. 2H26 營運動能來自 AMD Helios 伺服器專案開始放量：針對 Meta、Oracle 與 
OpenAI 等客戶的 AMD Helios 專案，已由去年的研發與小量送樣階段，於今
年正式啟動大量生產。儘管受限於保密協議無法透露單一客戶細節，但整體專
案規模與出貨量將迎來顯著成長。2026 年上半年營收約 41.0 億元，第三季單
季貢獻即達 40.3 億元 ， 推升前三季累計營收至 81.3 億元 。 受惠下半年強勁拉
貨，全年營收預估將突破百億大關並創下歷史新高，年增率上看五成。 
2. 與 NVIDIA 深化儲存專案合作，共同開發下次世代應用：跟 NVIDIA 除延續
多年來在人工智慧資料處理器 (Data Processing Unit, DPU AI) 儲存市場合作，
更於今年  GTC 大會深化與  NVIDIA 策略夥伴關係。雙方正緊密圍繞最新  
Vera Rubin 平台，共同開發下一代鍵值快取 (Knowledge Base Cache, KB Cache)  
在內容管理系統 (Content Management System , CMS ) 的應用。與 NVIDIA 次
世代合作案正處聯合開發階段，將聚焦提供高階 AI 儲存完整解決方案，其市
場份額與營收貢獻將隨 NVIDIA 的市場推廣逐步發酵。 
3. 2027 年既有業務穩健成長 ， 新興 AI 業務加速拓展：在三大事業部同步成長 ，
以及歐洲 、 東南亞 、 印度與中國等非北美市場大幅貢獻的帶動下 ， 預期 2027 年
仍將延續五成的強勁營收成長幅度。預期標準品、AI 儲存與 CSP 三大事業群
的營收年增率將呈現均衡且穩定成長。其中，標準品於各區域市場皆持續貢獻
穩定營收，AI 儲存受惠於整體應用規模擴增趨勢而由專責團隊推動成長，CSP 
專案則依既有進度維持穩健的營收佔比 。公司整體毛利率穩定維持在 25 ~ 30% 
區間，未來各項產品銷售與營運規劃，也將持續以穩住此毛利率水準作為首要
目標。 
4. 三大事業部門業務能見度佳，明年營運獲利可創新高：三大事業部同步成長，
Server & Storage 標準品業績規模持續擴大，出貨量顯著成長。AI Storage 出貨
快速攀升，掌握重點客戶成長商機。CSP 專案持續拓展，AMD Helios 進入大
規模量產階段 。合庫預估 2026 年營收 122.8 億元 、+63.4% YoY， 毛利率 28.2%。
營業利益 18.7 億元、+112.8% YoY，營業利益率 15.2%，歸屬母公司稅後淨利 
17.1 億元，每股盈餘 39.73 元。 
5. ESG (營邦在 2026 年上櫃公司治理評鑑結果為 81 ~ 100%)：為實踐企業社會
責任，並促進經濟、社會與環境生態之平衡及永續發展，營邦制定「永續發展
實務守則」 與 「永續資訊管理作業程序」 ，將永續做為公司營運決策的一環。透
過檢視治理、環境與社會的管理架構，營邦運用永續策略促進創新，以符合平
衡環境、社會及公司治理發展之國際趨勢，並透過企業公民擔當，提升國家經
濟貢獻 ， 改善員工 、 社區 、 社會之生活品質 ， 促進以永續發展為本的競爭優勢 。

## Document 221: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
좋은 실적이 상수인 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다. 이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 
이전 전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 26 년 3분기 
영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 상회한 
수준이다. 
실적이 끌고, 수급이 밀고 
지금까지는 높은 실적에 대한 불안보다는 먼 미래에 대한 불확실성을 앞서 주가에 
반영시키는 힘이 더 강하게 작용하다가, 주가가 1,190,000원을 찍은 이후 상승세를 
보이고 있다. 실적을 발표한 10월 28일도 10% 넘게 상승했다. 기대 이상의 실적과 
미국 반도체 시장 분위기가 좋았던 영향이다. 앞으로는 악재에 대해서 민감도가 떨
어지는 구간에 진입할 것으로 전망한다. 지나치게 하락한 주가와 밸류에이션이 버팀
목이 될 것이고, Anthropic의 IPO까지 발표되는 Big Tech 기업들의 실적은 시장의 
우려를 충분히 상쇄할 수 있을 것으로 기대한다. 추가로 진행되는 자사주 매수도 불
안한 수급을 해소할 수 있을 전망이다. 
목표주가는 괴리율을 고려해서 하향 조정 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 26년에도 AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 
시장은 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 
때문이다. 26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 
판단한다. 목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 
업황에 대한 우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 27년 EPS 
538,187원에 PER 6배를 적용하였다.

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기, 모든 악재를 잠재운 실적 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다.  이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 이전 
전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 
DRAM 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 35.4% 증가했다. B/G 는 +11.5%, ASP 는 
+28.2%로 추정했다. 3분기 DRAM 가격 상승률은 경쟁사 대비 높은 수준이다. Server 
DRAM 비중이 2분기 대비 높아진 것과 고객 믹스가 개선된 영향으로 추정한다. 
HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정한다. HBM이 3분기 가격에 미친 영향은 
제한적인 수준으로 판단한다.  
NAND 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 18.2% 증가했다. B/G 는 -1%, ASP는 +26%로 
추정한다. 이전 전망치를 상회했다. SSD 비중은 2분기 75%에서 3분기에는 77%로 
상승했다.  
26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 
상회한 수준이다. DRAM 영업이익은 지난 분기 대비 42.3% 증가한 65.1조원으로 
예상한다. 영업이익률은 전 분기 대비 4.0%p 상승한 83.0%로 추정한다. NAND 
영업이익은 전 분기 대비 23.1% 증가한 18.2조원으로 추정한다. 영업이익률은 전 
분기 대비 2.9%p 높아진 72.4%로 추정한다.

26년 4분기, DRAM 물량 효과 
SK하이닉스의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 29.4% 증가한 135.3조원으로 
예상한다. 가격상승은 지속될 것으로 예상하지만 26년 3분기 대비 상승폭이 낮아질 
전망이다. 물량은 3분기 대비 25% 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 신규 라인 가동 영향이다. 
4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 34.1% 증가한 111.7조원으로 예상한다. DRAM 
90.9조원, NAND 20.7조원이다.  
DRAM 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 36.4% 증가한 107조원으로 예상한다. ASP 는 
+15%, Bit Growth는 +25.0%로 가정하였다. HBM 출하는 지난 분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 예상하고, HBM4 비중은 60%대로 높아질 전망이다. 
26년 4분기 NAND 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 11.6% 증가할 것으로 예상한다. Bit 
Growth는 +5%로 전망하고, ASP는 +12%로 예상한다. SSD 비중은 소폭 개선될 
전망이다. SSD 내에서 eSSD 비중은 90% 수준이다.

자료: SK하이닉스, IBK투자증권

IBKS RESEARCH │5 
공정 전환 속도는 예상보다 빠르게 진행 중: 1cb nm, 321L 
SK하이닉스는 1c nm 비중을 빠르게 확대하고 있다. Wafer 투입이 제한된 상황에서 
공급 확대를 위한 최선의 방법이기 때문이다. HBM 수요는 1b nm를 중심으로 대응할 
예정이다. 이와 관련된 물량도 꾸준히 증가하지만, 출하 Bit 기준으로 1c nm 비중이 
26년 1분기 3% 수준에서 26년 3분기에는 20%, 연말에는 33%까지 높아질 전망이다. 
HBM이 아닌 DRAM 물량이 크게 증가할 전망이다. 이를 통해서 높아진 DRAM 수요 
대응할 것으로 예상한다. 26년 출하 Bit Growth는 28.5%로 상향되었다. 
NAND 생산라인의 공정 전환은 High-End로 꾸준히 변화 중이다. 321L 는 26년 
2분기에는 176L와 Cross over 되었다. 예상보다 1분기 빠른 속도다. 26년 3분기 321L 
비중은 50%이다. 26년말까지 321L를 64%까지 확대할 계획이다.

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
Check Point 
1. DRAM ASP의 차별화 
26년 3분기 DRAM ASP는 메이커별로 차별화가 크다. 26년 2분기 대비 상승폭은 마
이크론이 10% 후반, 삼성전자는 20% 초반으로 추정하고, SK하이닉스는 20% 후반이
다. LTA, HBM 등 다양한 변수가 있지만 SK하이닉스는 고객 비중 변화가 가장 큰 변
수라고 설명한다.  
2. HBM4 물량, 수율 문제 
26년 3분기에 가장 시끄러웠던 변수가 SK하이닉스의 HBM4 관련 품질 문제이다. 26
년 3분기 DRAM 매출과 영업이익으로는 문제가 있었다고 판단하기 어렵다. 26년 3분
기 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정되고 4분기 말에는 비중이 60%
까지 높아질 것으로 전망한다.   
3. HBM 8Hi 확대가 시장 규모 감소는 아니다. 
HBM4는 12Hi가 원래 제품 사양이다. HBM 공급이 부족해지면서 GPU를 좀 더 많이 
팔기 위해서 적용되는 HBM 용량을 줄이는 방향으로 제품 설계가 변경되는 중이다. 
2027년 하반기에는 8Hi 비중이 12Hi 비중을 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 하지만 이로 
인해서 HBM 전체 물량이 감소하는 것은 아닌 것으로 전망하고 있다.  
4. 투자와 생산은 다르다 
SK하이닉스는 용인팹을 공기를 앞당겨서 완공할 계획이다. 이로 인해서 공급량이 예
상 대비 커지는 것에 대한 우려가 있지만 건물 완공과 장비 반입에는 시차가 있고, 수
요를 고려하지 않은 장비 반입은 없을 것으로 밝혔다. 
5. 주주환원. 더 남아 있다 
공시를 통해서 26년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원의 주주환원 계획을 밝혔다. 배당을 30
조원 이상, 자사주 매입/소각을 60조원이상을 추진할 계획이다. 26년 4분기 중에 배
당은 14조원을 집행하고, 자사주는 20조원을 매입할 예정이다. 자사주 20조원 중에서 
10조원은 종업원 상여금 지급용이어서 주주환원에 포함되지 않는다.  
26년 4분기 실적 발표에서 추가적인 배당과 자사주 매입에 대한 계획을 발표할 것으
로 예상한다.

ASP 움직임은 메이커별로

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
펀더멘탈과 센티멘탈의 줄다리기 구간 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 시장은 
성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 
26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 판단한다.  
목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 업황에 대한 
우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 2027년 EPS 538,187원에 PER 
6배를 적용하였다.  
아직 제대로 보급되지 않은 Agent AI가 확산되면 컴퓨팅 파워 필요성에 대한 인식도 
크게 바뀔 것으로 전망한다. 메타의 MUSE는 가입자당 8GB의 DRAM을 가정하고 
있다. Open AI, Google, Anthropic 에서도 Agent Platform이 공개된다면 필요한 
DRAM은 폭발적으로 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 
메모리 수요에 대한 규모가 너무 커지는 것이 걱정이지 누가 살 것인지에 대한 것은 
걱정할 문제가 아니라 판단한다. 기존 AI 모델의 진화, 효율적 모델의 등장, Agent AI 
모델의 확대 모두 메모리 수요를 기하급수적으로 확장시키는 변수이다. 
투자 주체들의 자금난이 수요를 저해하는 변수는 아닌 것으로 판단한다. 대체 
투자자들은 지속적으로 증가할 것으로 전망하기 때문이다.  
메모리 업체들의 투자 계획은 수요에 대응하기 위한 투자일 뿐 공급과잉을 만들 
수준은 아닐 것으로 판단한다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

투자의견 안내 (투자기간 12개월)  투자등급 통계 (2025.10.01~2026.09.30)

한화에어로스페이스 (012450)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 방위산업 | 2026. 11. 2  
방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감

방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감 
지상방산 매출 1조 2,450억원으로 전년비 41%, 영업이익 217억원으로 전년비 
96.2% 급감하며 영업이익률은 1.7% 기록. 실적 급감에 대해 사측은 올해 주요 사업
의 납품 일정이 4분기에 집중됨에 따라 3분기 인도 물량이 적었던 것과 대전 사업장 
생산 중단에 따른 영향 때문이라고 설명. 단, 4분기에는 당초 계획에 따라 주요 사업의 
납품 물량이 집중되어 있어 실적이 개선될 것이고 대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 내에 정
상 가돌 하는 것이 목표라고 밝힘 
수주잔고 증가세 지속으로 성장 지속 가능할 것 
3분기 말 수주잔고 35조원으로 전년비 12.9% 증가. 이를 통한 지상방산 부문 성장 
지속 가능할 것으로 판단. 참고로 사측은 내년 매출 15% 수준 성장 전망 유지. 하지
만 금리 상승에 따른 글로벌 업종 평균 밸류에이션 하락에 따른 방산 부문 적용 멀티
플 하향 조정 그리고 상장 연결회사(한화오션, 한화시스템, 쎄트렉아이)와 상장 비연결
회사(한국항공우주)의 주가 하락 반영 영향으로 목표주가는 하향 조정 
8월 미 육군 차륜형 자주포 사업에서 시제 사업자로 선정. 향후 K9MH는 미 육군의 
작전 운용 개념에 맞춰 맞춤 조정을 거친 후에 특별한 결함 혹은 결격 사유가 없으면 
최종 계약 체결 예정. 동시에 미 육군의 궤도형 자주포 사업에도 참여할 계획. 뿐만 아
니라 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등으로 신시장 개척 역시 지속되고 있다는 점도 긍
정적 
 Analyst 이재광 
rheejaekwang @lssec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

## Document 252: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불리한 환경에서도 성장성 유지삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불리한 환경에서도 성장성 유지불리한 환경에_79fc85e4

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불리한 환경에서도 성장성 유지삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불리한 환경에서도 성장성 유지불리한 환경에_79fc85e4.pdf

시가총액 655,943억원

주가수익률 (%) 1개월 3개월 12개월  
 절대 1.9  2.4  (15.2)  
 상대 24.4  20.4  (43.5)  
 절대 (달러환산) 6.1  13.8  (8.6)

3Q26Re: 신규 시설 매출 인식 개시 
연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 6,401억원
(+1.0%, OPM 42.9%)로 컨센서스를 상회하는 실적 기록. 1~4공장이 완전 가동을 유지하는 
한편, 5공장은 PPQ 배치 생산 본격화되면서 매출 기여 확대. 상업 생산 전환이 예상되는 
2H27부터 이익 기여 커질 것으로 기대. 
연초 인수한 Rockville 공장은 3Q에도 안정적인 상업 생산을 이어갔으며 2Q 생산 물량의 
매출이 인식되며 매출 성장에 기여. 동사는 Rockville 공장 매출 인식 등을 반영하여 기존 
+15~20% 성장에서 +20%로 연간 가이던스 상향.   
고객사 수주 회복 기대 
3Q26 기준 누적 수주액은 219억 달러로 전분기 대비 소폭 증가했으나 과거 연간 수주액 
증가 대비 부족한 수준. 저조한 수주 실적은 지난해 초 의약품에 대한 관세 불확실성 증가로 
고객사들의 수주 문의가 저조했던 영향. 26년 초부터 관세 불확실성이 낮아지며 수주 문의
는 회복 추세. 약 1년 정도 논의 기간이 소요되는 것을 고려할 때 26년말~27년 초 수주 회
복 확인 가능할 것으로 기대. 
안정적인 공급망 유지가 중요한 CDMO 산업 특성상 노조 파업 가능성도 고객사 수주를 늦
추는 요인으로 판단. 다만, 10/16 인천 고등법원이 쟁의행위 가처분 항고심에 대해 인용 결
정을 하며 기존 3개 공정에서 9개 공정으로 확대되며 파업 우려는 다소 완화. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원 유지 
매수 의견과 목표 주가 200만원을 유지. 원달러 약세에도 불구하고 5공장 Ramp-up과 
Rockville 공장 매출 기여로 견조한 실적 성장 지속될 것으로 예상. 파업 이슈 해소와 대형 
수주 계약이 주가 상승을 이끌 것으로 판단.

삼성바이오로직스 (207940)

## Document 269: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 일회성 보상금 포함된 실적, ‘30년 ESS 수요 대비 배 이상 높은 셀 3사...LG에너지솔루션(373220) 일회성 보상금_7b277d22

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 일회성 보상금 포함된 실적, ‘30년 ESS 수요 대비 배 이상 높은 셀 3사...LG에너지솔루션(373220) 일회성 보상금_7b277d22.pdf
Report source: Hankyung

### Page 1

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Earnings Review Company Analysis | 이차전지/화학 | 2026. 11. 4  
일회성 보상금 포함된 실적, ‘30년 ESS 수요 대비 배 이상 높은 셀 3사 Capa 우려

AMPC 4,169억원 포함 영업이익 컨센서스 대폭 상회 
동사는 8일 매출액 9조 6,434억원(YoY 59%, QoQ 27%), AMPC 포함한 영업이익 
7,560억원(OPM 7.8%, YoY 26%, QoQ 567%)로 시장 Consensus(공정공시한 8일 
기준 3,264억원)을 대폭 상회하는 어닝 서프라이즈 발표. 회사는 ESS 출하 급증과 고
전압 Mid-Ni EV 파우치 확대, 견조한 EV향 2170-46시리즈 공급을 물량 중심을 성
장하였고, 폴란드 가동률 개선, 수익성이 견조한 원통형 물량 증가, 북미 ESS 생산성 
개선에 따른 고정비 부담 완화 및 고객사의 일회성 물량 보상금 영향으로 설명. 그러나 
일회성 물량 보상금의 구체적 금액, 반복 발생 여부는 제시하지 않아 불확실성 남겨. 
7,560억원의 영업이익에도 불구하고 영업외손실 9,872억원 발생으로 당기순손실 기
록. 이에는 소형 IT파우치 자산손상 반영되어, 가동률 개선 및 감가상각비 감소를 위해 
Capa를 조정하는 배터리 회사들의 대응 전략이 이번 실적에서 다시 확인

‘30년 ESS 수요 전망들 대비 국내사 Capa 배 이상 높아 
동사는 북미 ESS Capa 확대를 통한 탈중국 수요에 적극 대응하겠다는 전략. 파우치 
라인 추가 전환 및 신규 각형 라인 확보를 통해 북미 ESS Capa를 ‘26년말 50+에서 
‘27년 70+로 제시. 최근 타사에서 ‘26년 미국 ESS 수요를 80~100GWh, 혹은 
130+GWh로, ‘30년 두배 이상, 혹은 240+GWh로 수요 전망에 대해 답변한 바 있음. 
동사 전체 Capa 300GWh(‘25년 연말), SK On 179GWh, SDI 140GWh)임을 감안하
면 ‘30년 약 2배 수요를 가정하면 셀 3사 Capa의 32%에 달하는 수요, 만일 3배 수
요 가정하면 48.5% 수준. 북미 외 시장 가성비 중시 기조 및 중국 LFP 장악(‘25년 
97% M/S)으로 진입장벽 높음을 감안하면 미국 수요 중요. 이를 상쇄하기 위해 EV향 
배터리의 안정적인 공급 지속 필요하며, 이를 위해 동사 원통형은 TESLA의 비미국 시
장 판매량이, 파우치는 시장 자체 축소 지속 여부에 유의할 필요 
상대적으로 가장 견조한 출하량, 그러나 고평가 영역  
셀3사중 EV향 배터리 출하량 및 ESS 탑재에서 현재 가장 견조한 판매량을 보이고 있
으나, AMPC와 일회성 보상금이 영업손익의 상당부분을 견인한다는 점에서 수익성 취
약하다는 단점. 또한 ROE가 (-) 영역 임에도 PBR이 3.x내외로 거래되고 있어 고평가 
지적에서 자유롭기 어려워. 당분간 모회사인 LG화학의 PRS 발행에 따른 단기 오버행 
이슈도 관건. PBR 2.3 x(‘25년대비 하락하는 ROE 감안, 20% 할인)적용 목표주가 
290,000원 제시, HOLD 유지. 주식 투자 매력 낮은 상황으로 판단 
 Analyst 정경희 
khchung@ls-sec.co.kr

### Page 2

Company Report / LG에너지솔루션 / 2026. 11.4 
LS Securities Research 2 
EV향 3분기 data  
이차전지에서 EV향 비중은 여전히 가장 높다. 타입별로 보면 3분기 글로벌 EV향 이차전
지에서 각형의 M/S가 83%로 지속 증가하고 있으며, 동사가 생산하는 원통형과 파우치의 
경우 합산 M/S가 17%로 하락하였다. 현재 Wh 기준 동사 EV향 배터리에서 3분기 파우
치가 55%, 원통형이 45%로 파우치 비중이 10% 원통형을 상회한다. 
그림1 LGES 원통형, 파우치 타입 EV 향 분기별 탑재량 추이 
그림2

자료: SNE Research(2026.10), LS증권 리서치센터

그림 2 EV 향 폼팩터 M/S: 각형 확장 vs 원통형/파우치 감소  그림 3 동사 EV 배터리 타입 비중

자료: SNE Research(2026.10), LS증권 리서치센터  자료: SNE Research(2026.10), LS증권 리서치센터

SNE data에 따르면 글로벌 원통형 EV향 수요의 대다수가 TESLA에서 발생하고 있으며, 
‘24~’25년 역성장후 ‘26년 판매량 회복을 나타내는 TESLA 호조로 전년대비 LGES의 EV
향 원통형 판매량은 3분기 증가했다. 반면 파우치의 경우 ‘25년 파우치 탑재 EV 판매 호조
를 나타낸 현대기아차그룹 영향으로 YoY 탑재량이 증가했으나, 올해는 북미 OEM인 GM, 
Ford향 판매가 IRA 일몰로 전년대비 부진함에 따라 3분기 전년대비 25% 감소한 것으로 
나타난다.

그림 9 셀 3 사 분기별 EV 향 탑재량 글로벌 M/S 추이: M/S 감소중

자료: SNE 리서치, LS증권 리서치센터

밸류에이션, 수익성 대비 고평가 영역  
동사는 ‘24년 ROE -4.9%, ‘25년 -5.2%에 이어 ‘26년 -4.4%, ‘27년 -3.4%로 (-) 영역이 
지속되는 것으로 예상한다. ‘24년과 ‘25년 순이익은 (+)지만, 해외 합작회사가 많고 이들 이
익을 연결함에 따라 지배주주순이익은 적자를 기록한 것을 보인다. JV 규모가 축소되고 있
어 이러한 순이익과 지배주주 순이익상의 괴리는 좁혀지겠지만, ROE가 (-)영역 지속을 예
상함에 따라 현재 거래 PBR은 고평가 상황으로 볼 수 있다. 물론 이것은 향후 수익 성장
에 대한 시장 기대가 반영된 것으로 볼 수 있지만, 이차전지 6개사 평균 Consensus 대비 
실제 수익이 ‘23년부터 지속 하향되고 있다는 점, ESS대비 여전히 판매비중이 높은 EV에서 
출하량 정체가 일어나고 있다는 점, 미국 ESS에서 여전히 높은 중국 M/S를 감안하면 이러
한 기대 수준에 대해 다시 검토할 필요가 있다. 이에 더해, 모회사 기준에서는 필요한 과정
이지만 동사 주가 측면에서는 LG화학의 PRS 발행도 단기 오버행으로 당분간 주가에 부담
을 줄 수 있다.

그림 10 셀 3 사, 양∙음극재 3 사 추정치 변화율: ‘23 년부터 추정치 지속 하향 조정

자료: DataGuide, LS증권 리서치센터

미국 ESS가 모든 것을 해결할 수 있나? 
‘25년 글로벌 ESS 시장의 97%를 중국배터리사가 공급했다. LFP 각형이 표준화된 시장인 
것이다. 미국 시장만 중국에 대한 관세 허들, 정책 허들로 한국 배터리사에 기회로 작용한
다. 그러나 ‘26년부터 시작된 FEOC와 관세추가 부담에도 불구, 상반기 미국 ESS 시장의 
76%을 (기타사의 국적을 알 수 없기에 제외하더라도) 중국 셀사가 차지했다. 만일 한국 배
터리 유관회사들이 전망하듯 ‘30년 미국 ESS 시장이 160~200GWh 이상, 240+GWh, 혹
은 240~300GWh가 된다고 해도 3사 전체 Capa의 50%가 채 되지 못한다. EV 수요가 뒷
받침되지 않는다면 미국 ESS만으로는 가동률을 80~90% 이상 올리기 어렵다는 의미다. 특
히 동사는 원통형과 파우치를 생산하고 있으나, 원통형은 북미외 지역 TESLA의 판매에, 
파우치는 올해 출하량이 감소하는 것으로 나타나고 있다. 파우치 고객의 각형 채택 비중 
증가를 감안하면 동사 EV향 배터리 출하량 증가를 확신하기는 어렵다.   
긍정적 기대와 동시에 냉정한 점검이 필요한 시점으로 판단한다.

LS Securities Research 6 
표2 LG 에너지솔루션 3Q26 실적발표 주요 Q&A 
[Q&A]           
Q1-1. 4Q26 실적 전망과 2027년 큰 방향은? 
  A. 4Q26 ESS 출하량은 북미 수요 대응으로 QoQ 약 40% 증가 예상 
   유럽 고전압 Mid-Ni 파우치 및 원통형 물량도 연말까지 견조한 출하 지속 전망 
   3Q26 가동을 시작한 북미 JV 효과가 더해져 전사 매출은 QoQ 약 10% 성장 가능할 것으로 예상 
   가동률 개선과 사이트 안정적 램프업은 지속 추진하나 3Q26 일회성 요인 소멸의 기저효과 존재 
   4Q26 혼다 JV ESS CAPA 추가 가동 준비에 따른 초기 고정비 부담으로 수익성 일부 둔화 불가피

2027년 사업계획은 수립 중이며 전방 수요 변수로 구체적 가이던스 제시는 어려운 단계 
    ESS: 북미 확보 수주 기반으로 유의미한 물량 성장 지속 기대 
    소형: 전략 고객향 EV 원통형과 46 시리즈 신규 애플리케이션 판매로 안정적 성장 예상 
    EV 파우치: 중저가 제품 물량과 북미 EV 향 생산품이 더해져 성장 예상 
   전 사업부 매출 성장 기대하나 핵심 제품 경쟁력·기존 CAPA 효율화·가동률 극대화·원가 개선에 집중 
   2027년 매출 성장률·영업이익률 등 구체적 수치는 제시 X

Q1-2. 북미 PFE 요건 충족을 위한 LFP 공급망 전략과 중국 관련 주력 양극재 공급사 대응은? 
  A. 미국 생산세액공제 확보를 위해 직접재료비 내 PFE 소재 비중 요건을 충족하는 공급망 구축 필요 
   회사 설명상 PFE 소재 허용 비중은 2026년 40%에서 2027년 35% 이하로 강화. Non-PFE 비중과 혼동 유의 
   소재별 소싱 전략을 구체화해 준비 중이며 요건 충족은 차질 없이 진행될 것으로 판단 
   재료비 비중이 큰 양극재는 최근 계약한 국내 업체 외 복수 Non-PFE 업체 조달 옵션 확보

음극재·분리막·전해액도 기존 Non-PFE 공급망에 더해 신규 업체 적극 발굴 
   기존 원가 중심 최적화에서 정책·지정학 리스크를 고려한 공급망 안정성 강화로 전략 확대 
   협력사의 원가·기술·물량 대응력을 종합 평가하고 장기적으로 현지 협력사 발굴·현지화 추진 
   질문한 중국 관련 주력 공급사의 개별 지위·조달 비중에 대한 직접적인 상세 답변 X

Q2-1. ESS 사업의 수익성 전망 및 개선 방안은? 
  A. EV→ESS 라인 전환으로 수요에 맞는 생산체계를 구축하고 북미 고객 적기 공급에 주력 
   다수 사이트 동시 램프업에 어려움이 있었고 고정비 절감 노력에도 수익성 개선 속도는 시장 기대보다 느렸음 
   2026년 말 50GWh 목표 셀 라인 램프업은 마무리 국면. Link CAPA도 3Q26 에 이어 4Q26 추가 보강 예정 
   CAPA 안정화와 점진적인 고정비 부담 완화 기대 
   확보 CAPA 효율화, 소재 소싱 다변화로 원재료비·물류비 절감에 집중 
   AI 데이터센터 및 SI 프로젝트 비중 확대를 통한 고객 포트폴리오 최적화 추진 
   셀·시스템 고용량화로 원가 경쟁력을 높여 구조적 수익성 개선 추진 
   구체적 ESS 영업이익률 목표나 개선 폭은 제시 X

Q2-2. 북미 ESS 추가 수주 현황과 향후 모멘텀은? 
  A. 빠른 시장 성장 및 Non-PFE 에 우호적인 정책으로 북미 현지 생산 셀 수요 급증 
   현재 계획된 CAPA 기준으로 2028년까지 물량은 대부분 확정. 그 이후 물량 중심으로 고객 수주 협의 진행 
   기존 전력망·유틸리티 고객, 이들을 통한 AI 데이터센터 BESS 수요, 하이퍼스케일러 BTM 직접 구매 수요 포함 
   예상 수요가 공급 가능 CAPA를 초과해 전략적으로 중요한 프로젝트 중심으로 미래 잔여 CAPA 운영 계획 
   추가 고객명·신규 계약량 등은 제시하지 않음. 예상 수요와 확정 수주를 구분

Q3-1. 46시리즈의 구체적인 수주 현황과 향후 모멘텀은? 
  A. 기존 OEM 부터 EV 스타트업까지 이미 440GWh 이상 물량 확보 
   추가로 다수 글로벌 OEM 과 협의 중이며 가까운 시일 내 의미 있는 규모의 수주 성과 기대 
   제품 경쟁력·지역별 생산 역량으로 46 시리즈 선도 입지를 강화하고 고객 포트폴리오 확대 계획 
   440GWh는 확보 물량이며 연간 판매량이나 생산 CAPA가 아님. 추가 계약 규모·고객명은 미공개

### Page 7

Company Report / LG에너지솔루션 / 2026. 11.4 
LS Securities Research 7

Q3-2. Tabless 2170의 경쟁력과 수주·신규 시장 대응 현황은? 
  A. 기존 제품 대비 낮은 발열·저항으로 안전성 및 고출력에 유리해 HEV·BBU·로봇 등 다양한 용도에 적합 
   오창에서 전동공구용 제품은 이미 양산 중  
   BBU 향 LFP 2170은 2Q27 양산 목표로 준비 중 
   HEV 향은 2027년 말 애리조나에서 양산 계획  
   LFP 2170은 NCM 대비 열안전성 테스트에서 우수한 성능을 보여 고객의 긍정적 피드백 확보 
   고객 니즈·수요에 적극 대응해 신시장 경쟁력 확보 추진. 수주 규모 등 정량 정보는 제시하지 않음

Q4-1. 재무 건전성 개선 방안과 2027 년 자금 조달 계획은? 
  A. 선제 CAPA 투자 자금은 저리 정책자금 장기차입 및 원화·외화 회사채로 안정적으로 조달해 왔음 
   주요 투자는 대부분 집행됐고 CAPEX는 2025년을 기점으로 유의미하게 감소 중 
   향후 영업현금흐름 확대분을 차입금 상환에 우선 활용, 자산 유동화를 통한 추가 재원 확보도 병행 
   차입금·금융비용 부담을 축소해 재무지표 개선 및 건전성 확보 추진 
   2026년 본사 추가 차입 계획은 없으며 해외법인은 기존 약정 차입금 일부 추가 인출 예정 
   2027년은 기존 차입금 차환·운전자금 범위에서 최소한의 자금 조달 계획 
   영업현금 확대·투자 부담 완화에 따른 잉여 재원으로 차입금 감축 기조 지속. 구체적 조달 규모는 미제시

Q4-2. 유럽 IAA 정책 방향에 대응한 중장기 점유율 확보 전략은? 
  A. IAA는 아직 최종안 미확정이나 수정 권고안상 외국인 직접투자·현지화 요건 강화 예상 
   배터리뿐 아니라 주요 소재의 현지 생산 역량 중요성이 확대될 전망 
   유럽 OEM 의 공급망 다변화로 현지 생산 체계를 갖춘 비중국 업체 소싱 니즈 확대, 경쟁 환경은 우호적 판단 
   폴란드 현지 양산 및 소재·부품 공급망을 이미 구축해 현지 배터리 소싱 수요에 신속 대응 가능 
   고전압 Mid-Ni 에 대한 유럽 고객 관심 증가. 폴란드 감가상각비 감소도 가격 경쟁력 확보에 기여 
   고전압 Mid-Ni 외 LFP·CTP 등 다양한 제품·솔루션으로 신규 수주를 확보해 유럽 점유율 확대 추진

자료: LS증권 리서치센터

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

participants’ attention focused on RBA Deputy

Governor Hauser's fireside chat. Offshore,
China GDP was the key release. "

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Private Sector Credit (%mth)
Oct 30, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5
Market f/c: 0.5, Range: 0.4 to 0.6
Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than
expected at 0.6%mth in August, above Westpac’s and the
market’s forecast for a 0.5%mth gain, leaving credit growth

running at 8.4%yr. Growth in housing credit continued to ease,
as we expected, though the mix highlights that the waning in
momentum is uneven, centred on investors rather than owner-
occupiers. Business credit meanwhile remains a source of
strength, notwithstanding some slowing recently.
For September, we expect credit growth to nudge to 0.5%mth,
with the divergence between housing credit and business
credit persisting.
AUS: Q3/Sep CPI (%qtr/%mth)

Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.6%, Westpac f/c: 1.3%,
Market f/c: 1.3%, Range: 0.6 to 1.4.
Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.4%, Westpac f/c: 0.6%,
Market f/c: 0.6 Range: 0.3 to 0.8
As noted in the review of the August CPI, our September
quarter CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr holding the
annual pace flat at 3.9%yr. At the margin there was a 0.04ppt
increase due to a lift in auto fuel but it still rounds to 1.3%.
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% highlighting that part

of the increase is due to usual seasonality.
In the month, the September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% with
the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Recreation & culture make
the largest contribution but transport is not far behind. Our
seasonally adjusted estimate increases 0.5%.
For more details please see “ September CPI Preview “.
AUS: Q3/Sep Trimmed Mean (%qtr/mth)
Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.8%, Westpac f/c: 0.9%,
Market f/c: 1.0%, Range: 0.9 to 1.0.

Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.3%,
Market f/c: 0.3%, Range: 0.2 to 0.4
Our September quarter Trimmed Mean estimate is unchanged
at 0.9%qtr with the annual pace dipping a touch from 3.6%yr
to 3.5%yr. There was a small downward revision of 0.03ppt to
0.88% compared to our original published estimate but it still
rounds to 0.9%qtr.
In the month of September we are looking for a 0.3% in
the Trimmed Mean holding the annual pace steady at 3.6%

which will be the fourth consecutive month at this pace. The
distribution of the September Trimmed Mean ranges from
–0.3% to 0.7%.
For more details please see “ September CPI Preview “.

NZ: RBNZ Official Cash Rate Review (%)
Oct 28, Last: 2.75, Westpac f/c: 3.00, Mkt f/c: 3.00
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. Data since September show a higher
outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

point, but increased risks to domestic consumption and
residential investment. On balance, the medium-term inflation
picture will probably be judged to have worsened slightly
given recent movements in energy prices and the exchange
rate. A vote seems very possible as differences in view will
likely remain among the MPC about how much the medium-
term inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.
Forward guidance will likely leave open the option to raise

the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy Statement
without presenting it as a sure thing.
NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has been gradually ticking higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result for
September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their
initial release.

### Page 9

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.9
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

## Document 311: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-13).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;
